   ac = fast_conv(a)
   m=ac.max()
   return ac,m
def lse_grad(a,beta):
   ac=fast_conv(a)
   z=beta*ac
   zm=z.max()
   e=np.exp(z-zm)
   lam=e/e.sum()
   # gradient of lse = lam? derivative of (beta^{-1} log sum exp) wrt c = lam. Actually if φ=(1/β) log sum exp(βc), dφ/dc=exp(βc)/sum = lam. So grad=2*grad_from_lam(lam).

We can compute h using real FFT:
```
def lag_cost(a, lam, size3):
    a_rep = np.zeros(size3); a_rep[:n]=a
    lam_rev = np.zeros(size3); lam_rev[:len(lam)] = lam[::-1]
    c = irfft(rfft(a_rep)*rfft(lam_rev))
    return 2.0 * c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
This computes convolution length 3n-1. Need ensure no circular wrap; size3 >=3n-1. Use next power of 2.

### Fast convolution
```
def nextpow2(m):
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()

def fast_conv(a):
    n = len(a)
    size = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, size)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
Potential negative tiny due FFT; for max no issue. Could `np.maximum(c,0)`? The true max nonnegative. For objective, max unaffected by small negative. For λ, negative tiny can be ignored? `np.maximum(ac,0.0)`. Use.

`def jac_from_lambda(a, lam):` returns grad wrt a for LSE where lam sums1? Actually gradient of φ = Σ λ_k c_k? If λ from softmax(βc) and φ = 1/β logsumexp, dφ/dc=λ. For max subgradient, gradient =2H. We'll use 2 in gradient but for tilt only need cost without 2. Define `lag_cost(a, lam)` returns h_i=Σ_j a_j lam_{i+j}. Then gradient of weighted sum Σλ_k c_k =2h. Good.

Implementation:
```
def lag_cost(a, lam):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    size = 1 << (3*n - 1).bit_length()
    ar = np.zeros(size); ar[:n]=a
    lr = np.zeros(size); lr[:L]=lam[::-1]
    c = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(ar, size) * np.fft.rfft(lr, size), size)
    return 2.0 * c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
Check if circular convolution correct size >= len(a)+len(lam)-1 = n + (2n-1)-1 = 3n-2. We used 3n-1, okay. Memory size for n=20000: nextpow2(59999)=65536. Good. For n=30000: 131072.

Could optimize by using only lam nonzeros? But fine.

### Objective
```
def fast_objective(a):
    a = np.maximum(a,0.0); s=a.sum(); if s<...; a=a/s
    n=len(a); c=fast_conv(a); m=c.max(); return 2.0*n*m
```
For internal keep normalized sum=1. Use `obj_and_conv(a)` returning obj,m,c maybe reuse. To avoid reconv in tilt line search, compute c for trial.

```
def eval_obj_from_c(a,c): return 2.0*len(a)*max(c)
```

def lse_value(c,beta):
   z=beta*c; zmax=z.max(); return zmax/beta + np.log(np.exp(z-zmax).sum())/beta
```
Gradient needs lam; can compute. For L-BFGS `gradient_a_from_lam(a,lam)`.

a = softmax(x)
c=fast_conv(a)
z=beta*c; zmax=z.max(); e=np.exp(z-zmax); lam=e/e.sum()
val=zmax/beta + np.log(e.sum())/beta
h=lag_cost(a,lam)  # this is gradient/2? dφ/da=2h
sigma = np.dot(2.0*h, a)

return val,g
```

### Tilt MW polish
Pseudo:
```
def tilt_polish(a0, time_limit, p_active=8, tau_list=(0.5,1,2,3,4), alpha_list=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0), floor=1e-6, verbose=True):
    a = norm_sum1(a0)
    best_a = a.copy(); best_obj = obj(a)
    n=len(a)
    t0=time.monotonic(); stall=0; tau=1.0; beta? use p
    while time < t0+time_limit and stall < max_stall:
       c = fast_conv(a); M=c.max(); obj=2nM
       if obj<best_obj: update best, stall=0 else stall++
       # weights
       cc = np.maximum(c,0)
       wbase = cc**p_active
       wsum=wbase.sum()
       if wsum<=0: break
       lam = wbase/wsum
       h = lag_cost(a, lam)
       hmin=h.min(); hmax=h.max(); range=hmax-hmin
       if range <= 1e-18: continue
       hnorm=(h-hmin)/range
       # Try tau values maybe around current
       best_trial_obj=obj; best_trial=a
       for tau in tau_candidates:
          a_tilt = a * np.exp(-tau*hnorm)
          a_tilt = np.maximum(a_tilt,0)
          st=a_tilt.sum()
          if st<=... continue
          a_tilt /= st
          # floor
          a_tilt = (1-floor)*a_tilt + floor*(1/n)
          for alpha in alphas:
             trial = (1-alpha)*a + alpha*a_tilt
             try_obj = objective_from_conv(trial)
             if try_obj < best_trial_obj - 1e-15: update
       if improved: a=best_trial; stall=0; tau maybe best_tau*1.2? else stall++
    return best_a,best_obj
```
However current a may not be normalized after trial; yes.

Could be okay limited. But many line search trials may be overkill. Reduce to max 6 trials: choose tau adaptively and alphas [0.1,0.5,1.0]. Start tau=1.0; after improvement maybe increase/decrease.

We can optimize by evaluating only alpha=1? Multiplicative a_tilt often already good. But if tau too high, need blend. We can first compute a_tilt for current tau and evaluate α in [0.1,0.3,0.7,1]. Then adjust tau based on best α.

Stall condition: If no improvement for e.g. 200 iters, restart: blend best with random perturbation or use alternative p. Instead of resetting a to best, perturb:
```
a = best_a + 0.02*rng.normal * best_a? clip normalize
```

def _softmax_obj(x, n, beta):

def _softmax_jac(x,n,beta):
   a=...; c=...; z...; lam=...; h=lag_cost(a,lam); g=2*h - a*np.dot(h,a) # since dφ/da=2h, sigma=2h dot a, grad_v=2h - a*(2h dot a)
   return g
```

### Detailed function `lp_fw_highs`
Pseudo code in final:
```
def lp_fw_highs(a0, time_limit, initial_K=250, max_K=600, add_per_iter=25, no_improve_limit=300, verbose=True):
    a = norm_sum1(a0); n=len(a)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_M=fast_conv(a).max()
    t0=time.monotonic(); noimp=0; t=0
    active = set(int(k) for k in np.argsort(fast_conv(a))[::-1][:initial_K])
    while time < t0+time_limit and noimp < limit:
       t+=1
       ac=fast_conv(a); M=ac.max()
       if M < best_M - 1e-12:
           best_M=M; best_a=a.copy(); noimp=0
       else: noimp+=1
       if noimp > 100 and t % 50 ==0:
           # reset active to top max_K from ac, maybe continue fewer
           active = set(... top max_K)
           noimp=50
       klist=sorted(active)
       if len(klist)>max_K: prune...
       g = solve_lp_direction(a,klist,n)
       if g is None: reduce; continue
       yg=fast_conv_xy(a,g); violations=yg > M + 1e-8; add top
       # line search
       alphas = [1.0/(t+1.0), 2.0/(t+2.0), 0.1,0.3,0.5,1.0]
       best_alpha=...
       # compute candidate for each alpha; also maybe alpha best from default with small 0.01
       a = best_trial
    return best_a,best_M
```

`solve_lp_direction(y,klist,n)`:
```
K=len(klist)
if K==0: return None
rows=[]; cols=[]; data=[]
for jj,k in enumerate(klist):
  ks=int(k); i_start=max(0, ks-n+1); i_end=min(n, ks+1)
  if i_start < i_end:
      cols += list(range(i_start,i_end)) # but using Python list extend okay
      rows += [jj]*(i_end-i_start)
      data += list(y[ks - np.arange(i_start,i_end)])
  rows.append(jj); cols.append(n); data.append(-1.0)
A = csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(K,n+1))
c = np.zeros(n+1); c[-1]=1.0
Aeq = csr_matrix(np.ones((1,n)), shape=(1,n+1)) # last 0
bounds = [(0.0,None)]*n + [(0.0,None)]
res=linprog(c,A_ub=A,b_ub=np.zeros(K),A_eq=Aeq,b_eq=[1.0],bounds=bounds,method='highs')
if res.status==0: g=res.x[:n]; g=max(0); s=sum; if s>0: return g/s
return None
```

### `norm_sum1` must not mutate input? Use copy.
```
def norm_sum1(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-15: return np.ones_like(a)/len(a)
    return a/s
```

def tilt_polish(a0, time_limit, p_values=(6,12,24), floor=1e-8, verbose=True):
    a=norm_sum1(a0); n=len(a); best=a.copy(); best_obj=fast_obj(a)
    rng=default_rng(...)
    t0=time.monotonic(); remaining=time_limit
    for p in p_values:
       if time>t0+time_limit: break
       sub_limit = min((time_limit - elapsed)/ (number of p left?) maybe just while elapsed < t0+time_limit*share)
       # Run with current a (or best if a worse)
       a = best.copy() if fast_obj(a)>best_obj? maintain running.
       tau=1.0; stall=0
       while elapsed < t0+time_limit and stall<150:
          c=fast_conv(a); M=c.max(); obj=2*n*M
          if obj < best_obj - tol: update best, a=best? Actually if running improved, keep a.
          else: stall+=1
          if stall>50: perturb a from best: a=best + small noise, normalize; stall=0
          # weights
          cc=np.maximum(c,0)
          # Maybe raise to p but avoid overflow: use log? p up to 24, c ~1e-4, cc^p tiny for non-peaks, underflow to 0 okay? If top c 1e-4^24=1e-96, underflow? 1e-96 >1e-308 okay; p=24 safe. n large c could 1e-4, ^24=1e-96; sum not zero. For n=20000 c~5e-5, ^24=6e-104 safe. p=40 maybe 1e-120 safe. Could use softmax(log(cc)*p) but cc zeros. Use `w = cc ** p` then max1 shift: `w = w / w.sum()`.
          if w.sum()==0 use top indices uniform.
          h=lag_cost(a,w)
          hmin,hmax. if range=0 break
          hnorm=(h-hmin)/range
          # Try a set of updates. Use tau around 1; if previous failed, tau*=0.7.
          best_trial_obj=obj; best_trial=a
          # tau_candidates = [tau*0.5, tau, tau*1.5, 2.0, 4.0]? ad
          for tau_try in [0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 3.5]:
             mult=np.exp(-tau_try*hnorm)
             at=a*mult; st=at.sum(); if st<... continue; at=at/st
             at=(1-floor)*at + floor/n
             # line search alphas maybe [1.0, 0.5, 0.2]
             for alpha in [1.0,0.5,0.2]:
                tr=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*at; st=tr.sum(); tr/=st; obj_tr=fast_obj_noconv? Need conv.

          if best_trial_obj < obj - tol:
              a=best_trial.copy(); tau=max(0.2,min(8,best_tau*1.1)); stall=0
          else:
              tau*=0.7; stall+=1
       after p set, a=best? continue.
    return best,best_obj
```

def coordinate_polish(a0, time_limit, rng_seed=11):
    a=norm_sum1(a0); n=len(a); best=...; c; p=12; 
    t0; stall=0
    while time< and stall<200:
       c=fast_conv(a); M=c.max(); obj; update if improved
       cc=c.copy(); w=cc**12; w/=sum
       h=lag_cost(a,w)  # derivative/2
       # source candidates: top 40 by a*h
       src_scores=a*h; srcs=np.argsort(src_scores)[::-1][:40]
       # dest candidates: bottom 40 by h (or scores) not source
       dests=np.argsort(h)[:40]
       improved=False
       for src in srcs[:10]:
          if a[src] < 1e-12: continue
          for dst in dests[:10]:
             if dst==src: continue
             # try fractions
             for frac in [0.01,0.03,0.1,0.2,0.4]:
                amt=a[src]*frac
                tr=a.copy(); tr[src]-=amt; tr[dst]+=amt; clip; normalize? Sum stays same if clip? If src zero? not. no need renorm but after clip maybe okay; renorm.
                obj_tr=fast_obj; if improves: a=tr; c? break all
          if improved break
       if improved: stall=0 else stall++
       if stall % 50 ==0: random move
    return best
```

```

### Testing `tilt_polish` failure modes
- `w = cc ** p` might underflow all to zero if c values very small? c min ~0, max ~1e-4, p=24 ->1e-96, sum over 13400 ->1e-92 >0. safe. p=100 ->1e-400 underflow. Use p<=50. We can use log-softmax for robust:
```
logw = p * np.log(np.maximum(cc, 1e-300))
logw -= logw.max()
w = np.exp(logw); w/=w.sum()
```

```
def softmax_lag_weights(c,p):
   cc=np.maximum(c,0.0)
   # Normalize by max to avoid large log? log(cc/p) okay.
   m=cc.max()
   if m<=0: return None
   scaled=cc/m
   vals=scaled**p? If scaled<=1, scaled^p underflow less? max1, min0. For p=32, min might underflow but okay; better log.
   logv=np.log(np.maximum(scaled,1e-300))*p
   logv-=logv.max()
   w=np.exp(logv); s=w.sum(); if s<=0: weights uniform over top? else return w/s
```
Since scaled max1, log≤0, max0, exp safe. For nonpeaks scaled 0.1^32=1e-32 safe. Good.

### Write helper functions detailed

`next_pow2_ge(m)`:
```
def next_pow2_ge(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
```

`fast_conv_same(a)`:
```
def fast_conv_same(a):
    n=len(a); size=next_pow2_ge(2*n-1)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,size)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    # remove tiny negatives? maybe np.maximum(c, -1e-300)
    return c
```
If a has tiny negative? norm ensures nonneg. Good.

`fast_conv_xy(x,y)` for two arrays same length n:
```
def fast_conv_xy(x,y):
   n=len(x); size=next_pow2_ge(2*n-1); A=rfft(x); B=rfft(y); return irfft(A*B,size)[:2*n-1]
```

`fast_obj_from_c(c,n): return 2.0*n*c.max()` (c nonneg).
`fast_objective_norm(a): a=norm_sum1(a); c=fast_conv_same(a); return 2*len(a)*c.max()`

`smooth_weights(c,p)`:
```
def smooth_weights(c,p):
   cc=np.maximum(c,0.0)
   m=cc.max()
   if m<=0:
       return None
   scaled=cc/m
   logv=np.log(np.maximum(scaled,1e-300))*float(p)
   logv-=logv.max()
   w=np.exp(logv)
   s=w.sum()
   if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       idx=np.argsort(cc)[::-1][:50]
       w=np.zeros_like(cc); w[idx]=1.0; return w/w.sum()
   return w/s
```

`lag_cost(a,w)`:
```
def lag_cost(a,w):
   n=len(a); L=2*n-1
   size=next_pow2_ge(3*n-1)
   ar=np.zeros(size); ar[:n]=a
   wr=np.zeros(size); wr[:L]=w[::-1]
   c=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(ar,size)*np.fft.rfft(wr,size), size)
   return 2.0*c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
Maybe for `w` length 2n, no. Good.

```

### `generate_families` return list of normalized arrays. Need rng. For speed, maybe generate unnormalized then norm after each? Because norm needed before objective. We'll generate list of unnormalized. Use no lambda.

```
def generate_families(n, rng, seed=1):
    out=[]
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)+1.0
    r=n+1.0-i
    out.append(np.ones(n))
    for p in (-1.0,-0.5,-0.3,-0.1,0.1,0.3,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0):
       out.append(i**p); out.append(r**p)
    for p in (-0.5,-0.3,0.3,0.5):
       for q in (-0.5,-0.3,0.3,0.5):
           out.append(i**p + 0.5*r**q)
           out.append(0.5*i**p + r**q)
           out.append(i**p * r**q)
    for c in (0.2,0.5,0.8):
       for s in (0.03,0.08,0.15,0.3):
          out.append(np.exp(-((x-c)**2)/(2*s*s)))
    for c1 in (0.2,0.3,0.5):
       for c2 in (0.5,0.7,0.8):
          out.append(np.exp(-((x-c1)**2)/(0.02)) + np.exp(-((x-c2)**2)/(0.05)))
    for f in (1,2,3,5):
       for ph in (0.0, np.pi):
          out.append(np.maximum(0.5+0.5*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*x+ph),1e-3))
    # sigmoid
    for c in (0.3,0.5,0.7):
       for s in (5,15,30):
          out.append(1.0/(1.0+np.exp(-s*(x-c))))
    # random smooth
    for _ in range(8):
        v=rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
        # smoothing via FFT lowpass
        f=np.fft.rfftfreq(n)
        amp=np.exp(-abs(f)*20.0)
        ph=rng.uniform(0.0,2*np.pi,len(amp))
        spec=amp*(np.cos(ph)+1j*np.sin(ph)); spec[0]=0
        v=np.fft.irfft(spec,n)+2.0
        out.append(v)
    # sparse
    for _ in range(8):
       M=int(max(20, (0.8+rng.uniform(0,1.6))*np.sqrt(n)))
       M=min(M,5000)
       pos=rng.choice(n,M,replace=False)
       if rng.random()<0.5:
           w=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(M))
       else:
           w=(np.arange(1,M+1,dtype=float))**(-0.5)
           rng.shuffle(w)
       a=np.zeros(n); a[pos]=w; out.append(a)
    # normalize unique-ish
    res=[]
    for v in out:
       a=norm_sum1(v)
       # skip if all close uniform? can't compare many. just res.
       res.append(a)
    return res
```
This can produce many (about 2*11 +16*? + etc ~100). For each length, evaluating 100 candidates conv length 20k maybe 100*0.01=1s plus lag? okay. But memory storing 100 arrays of length20k (16MB) if keep all; we can evaluate and not store all, update pool. We can generate and evaluate one by one.

`evaluate_and_add_candidates(n, rng, pool, best_state?)`:

   for a in generate_families(n,rng):

### LP FW code with shifted LP
Need `solve_lp_shifted(y, c, klist, n)`:
```
def solve_lp_shifted(y,c,klist,n):
   K=len(klist)
   if K==0: return None
   max_entries=4000000
   if K*(n+1) > max_entries: return None
   A=np.zeros((K,n+1), dtype=np.float64)
   rhs=np.zeros(K)
   idx_arr=None? 
   for jj in range(K):
       k=int(klist[jj])
       ist=max(0,k-n+1); ien=min(n,k+1)
       if ist < ien:
           A[jj, ist:iend] = y[k - np.arange(ist,iend)]
       A[jj,n] = -1.0
       rhs[jj] = c[k]
   cvar=np.zeros(n+1); cvar[-1]=1.0
   Aeq=np.ones((1,n+1)); Aeq[0,n]=0.0
   bounds=[(0.0,None)]*(n+1)
   bounds[-1]=(-1.0,None)
   try:
      res=linprog(cvar, A_ub=A, b_ub=rhs, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=[1.0], bounds=bounds, method='highs')
   except Exception:
      try: res=linprog(... method='revised simplex')
      except: return None
   if res.status in (0,3?) and res.x is not None: ...
```
Highs status 0 optimal, 3? iter limit? If status 0. If `res.status==0`; if not, maybe use incumbent? SciPy returns status 0 success. Could accept 3 if has x? But may be infeasible. We can if res.x not None and bounds satisfied? Use `if res.x is not None`.
Need `res.x[-1]` t. Return g and t.

Building `np.arange(ist,iend)` each row repeated; Can precompute arange? For each row length varies. Could do `col_idx = ist + np.arange(iend-ist)`; val y[k-col_idx]. Good.

`lp_fw_shifted` details:
```
def lp_fw_shifted(a0,time_limit,initial_K=300,max_K=500,add_per_iter=20,no_improve_limit=250,verbose=True):
   a=norm_sum1(a0); n=len(a)
   if n>9000 or not HAVE_SCIPY: return a, fast_obj
   best=...; c=fast_conv
   active=set(top initial_K)
   t=0; noimp=0; fail=0; tEAU? 
   while time < limit and noimp<limit:
      t+=1; c=fast_conv(a); M=c.max(); obj=2nM; update best
      # active prune/reset
      if len(active)>max_K: keep tight (M-c small) top
      klist=sorted(active)
      g,t_lp=solve_lp_shifted(a,c,klist,n)
      if g is None: fail+=1; if fail>5: break; continue
      fail=0
      yg=fast_conv_xy(a,g)
      delta=yg-c
      # add violations: top delta > t_lp + 1e-8 or top c
      viol=np.flatnonzero(delta > t_lp + 1e-9)
      if len(viol)>0: top indices sort delta desc
      else: top c indices not in active? 
      add to active
      # line search over alphas
      alphas=[2.0/(t+2.0), 1.0/(t+1.0), 0.1,0.3,0.5,1.0]
      # discretize unique
      best_trial=a; best_trial_obj=obj
      for alpha in alphas:
          tr=(1-alpha)*a+alpha*g; tr=np.maximum(tr,0); s=tr.sum(); if s<... continue; tr/=s
          obj_tr=fast_obj
          if obj_tr < best_trial_obj - tol: update
      if best_trial_obj < obj - tol:
          a=best_trial.copy(); noimp=0
      else:
          # maybe use very small alpha default? could core. If no improve, noimp++
          noimp+=1
          if noimp%50==0: reset active to top max_K from c; a=best.copy()? Maybe if stuck, set a=best; continue.
   return best,best_obj
```
Line search 6 conv per iter. If LP solve 0.2s, total maybe 0.25s, 1000 iter in 250s. Good.

Potential bug with alpha=1.0 if g has zeros and produces zero mass? g sum1. okay.

### L-BFGS final
`lbfbs_polish(a0,time_limit,beta=80)`:
```
def lbfbs_polish(a0,time_limit,beta=80.0,maxfun=400):
   if not HAVE_SCIPY: return a0,obj
   a=norm_sum1(a0); n=len(a)
   x0=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-12))
   try:
      res=minimize(_lbfbs_fun, x0, args=(n,beta), jac=_lbfbs_jac, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxfun':maxfun,'maxiter':max(20,maxfun//2),'iprint':0})
   except Exception: return a,obj
   if res.x is not None:
       a2=softmax(res.x); obj=fast_obj; return better
```
Maxfun is function evaluations; each eval calls fun and jac separate (two conv each). If maxfun=300, ~1200 FFT, for n=20k maybe 12s? okay. Set lower maxfun for large n: `maxfun = 200 if n>10000 else 350`.

Need `_lbfbs_fun` and `_lbfbs_jac` top-level. They use `softmax_vec` and fast_conv/lag_cost. Function signature must accept args.

```
def softmax_vec(x): x=x-x.max(); y=np.exp(x); s=y.sum(); if s...; return y/s

def _lbfbs_fun(x,n,beta):
    a=softmax_vec(x)
    c=fast_conv_same(a)
    z=beta*c
    zmax=z.max()
    e=np.exp(z-zmax)
    s=e.sum()
    if s<=0: return 1e100
    return float(zmax/beta + np.log(s)/beta)

def _lbfbs_jac(x,n,beta):
    a=softmax_vec(x)
    c=fast_conv_same(a)
    z=beta*c; zmax=z.max(); e=np.exp(z-zmax); s=e.sum(); lam=e/s
    h=lag_cost(a,lam)
    ga=2.0*h
    sig=float(np.dot(ga,a))
    return ga - a*sig
```
Should we avoid recomputing c twice? No. For speed, `_lbfbs_jac` could return inf if NaN. L-BFGS may call with x that yields overflow? softmax safe.

If L-BFGS changes n? no.

### coordinate_polish implementation
Use `smooth_weights` with p=12. We need `fast_objective_norm` in loops. To avoid recomputing c after each accepted move? We need objective trial. After accepted, we could set a=trial and continue; c not known but next loop computes. Fine.
```
def coordinate_polish(a0,time_limit,rng_seed=5):
   a=norm_sum1; n; best; best_obj; stall=0; rng=default_rng
   while time<limit and stall<200:
      c=fast_conv_same(a); M=c.max(); obj=...; update best
      w=smooth_weights(c,12); if w is None break
      h=lag_cost(a,w)
      sources=np.argsort(a*h)[::-1][:12]
      # destinations: global min h, maybe top low
      dest = int(np.argmin(h))
      if dest in sources? find next
      improved=False
      for src in sources:
         src=int(src)
         if a[src] <= 1e-13: continue
         # choose dst with min h excluding src
         h2=h.copy(); h2[src]=np.inf? No copy expensive. Use argsort low 10.
         for dst in np.argsort(h)[:10]:
            dst=int(dst)
            if dst==src: continue
            for frac in (0.01,0.04,0.1,0.25):
                amt=a[src]*frac
                tr=a.copy(); tr[src]-=amt; tr[dst]+=amt
                tr=np.maximum(tr,0); s=tr.sum(); if s<=... continue; tr/=s
                obj_tr=fast_obj
                if obj_tr < obj - 1e-10: # use current obj not best? ensure improve from current
                    a=tr; obj=obj_tr; improved=True; break
            if improved: break
         if improved: break
      if improved:
         stall=0
         if fast_obj(a) < best_obj: update? We already compare to current but current may not be best. Use try_update.
      else:
         stall+=1
         if stall>100:
             # random move or reset to best
             a=best_a.copy() + small noise; normalize
             stall=0
   return best_a,best_obj
```
Need `best_a` variable for reset. `fast_obj(a)` again expensive. We can update best in try_update after improved. But inside we only know `obj_tr`; compare to `best_obj`: if better update best. If not better but improved current maybe still baseline. Use `if obj_tr < best_obj - tol: best...`. Current obj may be >best if after perturb. Good.

In trial, we should not renormalize if sum unchanged; because dividing by sum after clipping? No clipping needed; but if src amt negative? no. Use `tr = a.copy(); tr[src]-=amt; tr[dst]+=amt; s=tr.sum(); if s<...; tr/=s` safe. Efficiency: a.copy per trial. 12*10*4=480 copies per no-improve sweep, maybe too many. Reduce: sources top5, dest top3, fractions3 =>45. Good.

Use `current_obj` variable to compare trial for improvement from current, but best update separately.

`stall` reset when best improves? Maybe if any trial improves current but not best, still progress. Use current.

### Try to estimate if LP with shifted direction will improve
Potential issue with active set: We need compute current c full; `M = c.max()`. Prune active by `M-c` keep tight. But in shifted LP, constraints with lower c are looser; maybe active set should include not only tight but those where direction may violate. Add violations from `delta = yg - c > t_lp`. Good.
If LP fails due infeasible? The constraints can always be satisfied for some t (upper), so feasible. Lower t bound -1 may be infeasible? We minimize; lower bound just variable bound not constraint. In LP, variable t lower -1; if Optimal would be < -1, but true t cannot be < -1? c≤1, yg≥0 => yg-c≥-1. Yes lower -1 valid. Feasible with t large? We only have lower, no upper; but objective minimize, constraints. There is always solution g uniform gives finite yg-c. The LP is bounded below by -1; feasible. Good.

Line search: If LP direction good, objective decreases. If not, may no improve. Could use `a_new = (1-alpha)*best + alpha*g` rather than current a? Current a may have worse than best if perturbations; line search should start from best to avoid degradation. In FW loop, `a` is current; if no improve, we can set a=best.copy() before direction? But direction based on best. In previous no improve stops. Let's in `lp_fw_shifted` always start each iteration from current a; if no improve many, set `a=best_a.copy()` and reset active? ### Potential issue: `fast_conv_same` using A*A (not conjugate) for real sequences is correct for ordinary convolution? FFT of real multiplied by itself (not conjugate) yields circular convolution of a with a (linear if zero-padded). For linear convolution, if X=FFT(x), Y=FFT(y), IFFT(X*Y) = circular convolution x*y (ordinary product with exponents sum). For real not conjugate, correct. Good.

### `tilt_polish` top-level implementation
Need pass `t_global`? It uses time.monotonic internally and time_limit. No need t0 global except print. It can print elapsed.
```
def tilt_polish(a0, time_limit, p_values=(8,16), floor=1e-8, verbose=False):
    a=norm_sum1(a0); n=len(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_obj=fast_objective_normalized(a)
    t0=time.monotonic()
    rng=np.random.default_rng(987)
    tau=1.0
    for p in p_values:
       if time.monotonic()-t0 >= time_limit: break
       sub_t = time_limit - (time.monotonic()-t0)
       # maybe reset a to best if current worse
       if a is None or fast_objective_normalized(a) > best_obj + 1e-9:
           a=best.copy()
       stall=0
       while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit and stall < 120:
           c=fast_conv_same(a)
           m=c.max(); obj=2.0*n*m
           if obj < best_obj - 1e-11:
               best_obj=obj; best=a.copy(); stall=0
           else:
               stall += 1
           if stall > 40:
               # perturb best
               base=best.copy()
               noise=rng.normal(0.0,0.02,n)*base
               add = base + noise
               add = 0.99*add + 0.01*(1.0/n)
               a=norm_sum1(add)
               stall=0
               continue
           w=smooth_weights(c,p)
           if w is None: break
           h=lag_cost(a,w)
           hmin=float(h.min()); hmax=float(h.max()); rng_h=hmax-hmin
           if not np.isfinite(rng_h) or rng_h < 1e-30: break
           hnorm=(h-hmin)/rng_h
           # ensure hnorm finite
           hnorm=np.clip(hnorm,0.0,1.0)
           best_trial_obj=obj; best_trial=a; best_step=None
           # trials list generated dynamically
           trials=[]
           trials.append((tau,1.0))
           trials.append((tau*0.5,1.0))
           trials.append((tau*2.0,0.6))
           trials.append((tau*3.0,0.3))
           for tau_try,alpha in trials:
               if tau_try <= 1e-6: continue
               tr=(1.0-alpha)*a
               if alpha !=1.0:
                   mult=np.exp(-tau_try*hnorm)
                   at=a*mult; s=at.sum(); if s<... continue; at=at/s
                   tr=tr + alpha*at
               else:
                   mult=np.exp(-tau_try*hnorm); at=a*mult; s=at.sum(); if s<... continue; at=at/s; tr=at
               # floor
               tr=(1.0-floor)*tr + floor*(1.0/n)
               tr=np.maximum(tr,0.0); s=tr.sum(); if s<... continue; tr/=s
               c_t=fast_conv_same(tr); obj_t=2.0*n*c_t.max()
               if obj_t < best_trial_obj - 1e-11:
                   best_trial_obj=obj_t; best_trial=tr.copy(); best_step=(tau_try,alpha)
           if best_trial_obj < obj - 1e-11:
               a=best_trial
               if best_step[1] < 0.9 and best_step[0] > tau:
                   tau = min(8.0, best_step[0]*1.1)
               elif best_step[0] < tau:
                   tau = max(0.2, best_step[0]*0.8)
               stall=0
           else:
               tau = max(0.1, tau*0.6)
               stall += 1
       a=best.copy()
    return best,best_obj
```
In trial alpha=1, `tr=at` and floor. For alpha<1, we compute base `(1-alpha)*a` then add. But `at` normalized; blend sums 1 if floor? We then normalize after. Good.
But for alpha=1, after floor, `tr` sum maybe 1; normalize.
If `hnorm` large? clipped.

Throughput: Each iteration 4 conv (trials) + h lagcost. If 300 iter, 1200 conv +300 lagcost. For n=6700, maybe 6-10s? Actually rfft 16384 maybe 0.003s, 1500*0.003=4.5s. Good. Could run longer.

Parameter p_values: maybe (6,12,24,40). More time. Use p_values=(6,12,32). For final, maybe.

Stall max 120 per p; if no improve, perturb and continue. If p loop exhausted, return. Good.

`lp_fw_shifted`:
```
def lp_fw_shifted(a0,time_limit,initial_K=300,max_K=600,add_per_iter=25,no_improve_limit=250,verbose=False):
  a=norm_sum1; n; if n>9000 or not HAVE_SCIPY: return a, fast_obj
  best=a; best_obj; c=fast_conv
  active=set(... top initial_K)
  noimp=0; fail=0; t=0
  while time<t0+time_limit and noimp<limit:
    t+=1
    c=fast_conv_same(a); M=c.max(); obj=2nM
    if obj < best_obj -1e-11: update best, noimp=0 else noimp+=1
    if noimp>80:
       # reset active to top max_K of c, maybe if repeatedly set a=best
       active=set(top max_K c)
       if noimp > 120: a=best.copy(); continue
    # prune active
    if len(active)>max_K:
       margins=np.array([M - c[int(k)] for k in active])
       keep_idx=np.argsort(margins)[:max_K]
       active=set(int(list(active)[i]) for i in keep_idx) # no inner? use list
    klist=sorted(active)
    g,t_lp=solve_lp_shifted(a,c,klist,n)
    if g is None: fail+=1; if fail>6: break; continue
    fail=0
    yg=fast_conv_xy(a,g)
    delta=yg-c
    # add violations top
    add_idx = np.argsort(delta)[::-1][:add_per_iter]
    for idx in add_idx:
       if int(idx) not in active: active.add(int(idx))
    # Also ensure top c lags active
    topc=np.argsort(c)[::-1][:20]
    for idx in topc: active.add(int(idx))
    # line search
    alphas_np = np.array([2.0/(t+2.0), 1.0/(t+1.0), 0.1,0.25,0.5,0.9,1.0])
    best_trial_obj=obj; best_trial=a
    for alpha in alphas_np:
       alpha=float(max(0.0,min(1.0,alpha)))
       tr=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*g
       tr=np.maximum(tr,0); s=tr.sum(); if s<... continue; tr/=s
       obj_t=fast_objective_normalized(tr)
       if obj_t < best_trial_obj - 1e-11: update
    if best_trial_obj < obj - 1e-11:
       a=best_trial; noimp=0
       if verbose and t%50==0: print
    else:
       noimp +=1
  return best,best_obj
```
Need avoid modifying active while iterating? fine.
When prune: `active_list = list(active)`; margins array; keep. Use no lambda.
Line search alpha list compute each iter cheap.

Potential issue: `fast_objective_normalized(tr)` recomputes norm; tr sum1. Could use `2*n*fast_conv_same(tr).max()`. But function fine.

def scan_length(n, rng, best_seq, best_obj, t0, deadline, pool):
    if time.monotonic() > deadline: return best_seq,best_obj

### `add_pool` maintaining worst:
```
def add_pool(pool, a, obj, max_pool=10):
    if a is None or not np.isfinite(obj): return pool
    if len(pool) < max_pool:
       pool.append((a.copy(), float(obj)))
    else:
       worst_idx = -1; worst_val = float('inf')
       for i in range(len(pool)):
           if pool[i][1] < worst_val:
               worst_val=pool[i][1]; worst_idx=i
       if worst_idx >=0 and obj < worst_val:
           pool[worst_idx] = (a.copy(), float(obj))
    return pool
```
Good.

`get_best_candidates(pool, k, extra_seq=None)`:
```
def get_best_candidates(pool, k, extra_seq=None, extra_obj=float('inf')):
    items=list(pool)
    if extra_seq is not None:
       items.append((extra_seq.copy(), extra_obj))
    res=[]
    while len(res) < k and items:
       bi=0; bv=1e100
       for i in range(len(items)):
           if items[i][1] < bv:
               bv=items[i][1]; bi=i
       res.append((items[bi][0], items[bi][1]))
       items.pop(bi)
    return res
```

def softmax_vec(x):
    y=np.exp(x-x.max())
    s=y.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite:
       return np.ones(len(x))/len(x)
    return y/s
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a))^2
under the 1800-second budget.

Strategy:
- Start from any provided height_sequence_1 and diverse parametric/random initializations.
- Use fast FFT-based convolution for all inner objective evaluations.
- Combined local optimization:
  * multiplicative-weights / smoothed-subgradient tilting
  * Frank-Wolfe-like LP directions with shifted linearization, solved by HiGHS if available
  * coordinate-mass transfers
  * L-BFGS on a log-sum-exp smoothing of the peak
- Scale promising shapes to nearby lengths.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog, minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False


def next_pow2_ge(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def norm_sum1(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-15:
        return np.ones_like(a, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def fast_conv_same(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(1, dtype=np.float64)
    size = next_pow2_ge(2 * n - 1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, size)
    c = c[:2 * n - 1]
    return c


def fast_conv_xy(x, y):
    n = len(x)
    size = next_pow2_ge(2 * n - 1)
    X = np.fft.rfft(x, size)
    Y = np.fft.rfft(y, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(X * Y, size)
    return c[:2 * n - 1]


def fast_objective_normalized(a):
    c = fast_conv_same(a)
    m = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * len(a) * m)


def add_pool(pool, a, obj, max_pool=10):
    if a is None or not np.isfinite(obj):
        return pool
    if len(pool) < max_pool:
        pool.append((a.copy(), float(obj)))
    else:
        worst_idx = -1
        worst_val = float("inf")
        for i in range(len(pool)):
            if pool[i][1] < worst_val:
                worst_val = pool[i][1]
                worst_idx = i
        if worst_idx >= 0 and obj < worst_val:
            pool[worst_idx] = (a.copy(), float(obj))
    return pool


def update_with_obj(best_seq, best_obj, a, obj, label, t0):
    if a is None or not np.isfinite(obj):
        return best_seq, best_obj
    a = norm_sum1(a)
    if best_seq is None or obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
        best_seq = a.copy()
        best_obj = float(obj)
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
        print("NEW BEST [" + label + "] obj=" + repr(best_obj) +
              " n=" + str(len(a)) + " t=" + repr(elapsed) + "s")
    return best_seq, best_obj


def try_update_best(best_seq, best_obj, a, label, t0):
    if a is None:
        return best_seq, best_obj
    a = norm_sum1(a)
    obj = fast_objective_normalized(a)
    return update_with_obj(best_seq, best_obj, a, obj, label, t0)


def get_best_candidates(pool, k, extra_seq=None, extra_obj=float("inf")):
    items = []
    for a, obj in pool:
        items.append((a, obj))
    if extra_seq is not None and np.isfinite(extra_obj):
        items.append((extra_seq, float(extra_obj)))
    res = []
    while len(res) < k and items:
        bi = 0
        bv = float("inf")
        for i in range(len(items)):
            if items[i][1] < bv:
                bv = items[i][1]
                bi = i
        res.append((items[bi][0], items[bi][1]))
        items.pop(bi)
    return res


def interpolate_sequence(f, new_n):
    old_n = len(f)
    new_n = int(new_n)
    if old_n == new_n:
        return f.copy()
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s < 1e-15:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / float(new_n)
    f = f / s
    old_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, old_n)
    new_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, new_n)
    out = np.interp(new_x, old_x, f)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    ss = float(out.sum())
    if ss < 1e-15:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / float(new_n)
    return out / ss


def generate_families(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    out = []
    if n <= 0:
        return out

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
    r = (n + 1) - np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)

    out.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    powers = (-1.0, -0.7, -0.5, -0.3, -0.1, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0)
    for p in powers:
        out.append(i ** p)
        out.append(r ** p)

    mixed_powers = (-0.7, -0.5, -0.3, 0.3, 0.5)
    for p in mixed_powers:
        for q in mixed_powers:
            out.append(i ** p + 0.5 * (r ** q))
            out.append(0.5 * (i ** p) + r ** q)
            out.append((i ** p) * (r ** q))
            out.append((i ** p) + (r ** q))

    for c in (0.15, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.85):
        for s in (0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.18, 0.35):
            out.append(np.exp(-((x - c) ** 2) / (2.0 * s * s)))

    for c1 in (0.15, 0.3, 0.5):
        for c2 in (0.5, 0.7, 0.85):
            out.append(np.exp(-((x - c1) ** 2) / 0.02) + np.exp(-((x - c2) ** 2) / 0.06))

    for f in (1, 2, 3, 5):
        for ph in (0.0, np.pi):
            out.append(np.maximum(0.5 + 0.5 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f * x + ph), 1e-3))

    for c in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75):
        for s in (5.0, 12.0, 25.0, 50.0):
            out.append(1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-s * (x - c))))

    # Smooth random Fourier fields.
    n_rand = 8 if n <= 10000 else 4
    for _ in range(n_rand):
        fqs = np.fft.rfftfreq(n)
        amp = np.exp(-abs(fqs) * 18.0)
        ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, len(amp))
        spec = amp * (np.cos(ph) + 1j * np.sin(ph))
        spec[0] = 0.0
        v = np.fft.irfft(spec, n) + 2.0
        out.append(v)

    # Sparse starts.
    n_sparse = 10 if n <= 10000 else 6
    for _ in range(n_sparse):
        m = int(max(16, (0.8 + rng.uniform(0.0, 1.8)) * np.sqrt(n)))
        m = min(m, 3000)
        pos = rng.choice(n, m, replace=False)
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            w = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64))
        else:
            w = (np.arange(1, m + 1, dtype=np.float64)) ** (-0.6)
            rng.shuffle(w)
            w = w / w.sum()
        v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        v[pos] = w
        out.append(v)

    res = []
    for v in out:
        a = norm_sum1(v)
        if a.size == n:
            res.append(a)
    return res


def scan_length(n, rng, best_seq, best_obj, t0, deadline, pool):
    if time.monotonic() > deadline:
        return best_seq, best_obj
    n = int(n)
    fam = generate_families(n, rng)
    label = "scan_n" + str(n)
    for a in fam:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        a = norm_sum1(a)
        obj = fast_objective_normalized(a)
        if np.isfinite(obj):
            add_pool(pool, a, float(obj), 10)
            best_seq, best_obj = update_with_obj(best_seq, best_obj, a, float(obj), label, t0)
    return best_seq, best_obj


def smooth_weights(c, p):
    cc = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    m = float(cc.max())
    if m <= 0.0:
        return None
    scaled = cc / m
    logv = p * np.log(np.maximum(scaled, 1e-300))
    logv -= logv.max()
    w = np.exp(logv)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        idx = np.argsort(cc)[::-1][:64]
        w = np.zeros_like(cc, dtype=np.float64)
        if len(idx) == 0:
            return None
        w[idx] = 1.0
        return w / w.sum()
    return w / s


def lag_cost(a, w):
    n = len(a)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    size = next_pow2_ge(3 * n - 1)
    ar = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
    ar[:n] = a
    wr = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
    wr[:L] = w[::-1]
    conv = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(ar, size) * np.fft.rfft(wr, size), size)
    return 2.0 * conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1]


def tilt_polish(a0, time_limit, p_values=(8, 16, 32), floor=1e-8, verbose=False):
    time_limit = max(1e-6, float(time_limit))
    a = norm_sum1(a0)
    n = len(a)
    c0 = fast_conv_same(a)
    best_obj = float(2.0 * n * c0.max())
    best = a.copy()

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    tau = 1.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(987654)

    for p in p_values:
        if time.monotonic() - t0 >= time_limit:
            break
        if fast_objective_normalized(a) > best_obj + 1e-8:
            a = best.copy()

        stall = 0
        while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit and stall < 140:
            c = fast_conv_same(a)
            m = float(c.max())
            obj = float(2.0 * n * m)

            if obj < best_obj - 1e-11:
                best_obj = obj
                best = a.copy()
                stall = 0
            else:
                stall += 1

            if stall > 40:
                base = best.copy()
                noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.02, n) * base
                add = base + noise
                add = 0.99 * add + 0.01 / float(n)
                a = norm_sum1(add)
                stall = 0
                continue

            w = smooth_weights(c, p)
            if w is None:
                break

            h = lag_cost(a, w)
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
                break
            hmin = float(h.min())
            hmax = float(h.max())
            span = hmax - hmin
            if span < 1e-30:
                break
            hnorm = (h - hmin) / span
            hnorm = np.clip(hnorm, 0.0, 1.0)

            best_trial_obj = obj
            best_trial = a
            best_step = (tau, 1.0)

            trials = (
                (tau, 1.0),
                (0.5 * tau, 1.0),
                (2.0 * tau, 0.6),
                (3.0 * tau, 0.25),
            )

            for tau_try, alpha in trials:
                if tau_try <= 1e-8:
                    continue
                mult = np.exp(-float(tau_try) * hnorm)
                at = a * mult
                sat = float(at.sum())
                if sat < 1e-15:
                    continue
                at = at / sat
                at = (1.0 - floor) * at + floor / float(n)
                at = np.maximum(at, 0.0)
                sat = float(at.sum())
                if sat < 1e-15:
                    continue
                at = at / sat

                if alpha >= 0.999:
                    tr = at
                else:
                    tr = (1.0 - alpha) * a + alpha * at
                    tr = np.maximum(tr, 0.0)

                tr = (1.0 - floor) * tr + floor / float(n)
                tr = np.maximum(tr, 0.0)
                st = float(tr.sum())
                if st < 1e-15:
                    continue
                tr = tr / st

                c_t = fast_conv_same(tr)
                obj_t = float(2.0 * n * c_t.max())
                if obj_t < best_trial_obj - 1e-11:
                    best_trial_obj = obj_t
                    best_trial = tr.copy()
                    best_step = (float(tau_try), float(alpha))

            if best_trial_obj < obj - 1e-11:
                a = best_trial
                st_tau = best_step[0]
                if st_tau > tau * 1.2:
                    tau = min(8.0, st_tau * 1.15)
                elif st_tau < tau * 0.8:
                    tau = max(0.15, st_tau * 0.85)
                stall = 0
            else:
                tau = max(0.1, tau * 0.65)
                stall += 1

        a = best.copy()

    return best, best_obj


def solve_lp_shifted(y, c, klist, n):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    K = len(klist)
    if K <= 0:
        return None
    if K * (n + 1) > 6000000:
        return None

    A = np.zeros((K, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    rhs = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)

    for jj in range(K):
        kk = int(klist[jj])
        ist = max(0, kk - n + 1)
        ien = min(n, kk + 1)
        if ist < ien:
            cols = np.arange(ist, ien, dtype=np.int64)
            A[jj, ist:ien] = y[kk - cols]
        A[jj, n] = -1.0
        rhs[jj] = max(0.0, float(c[kk]))

    obj = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    obj[-1] = 1.0

    Aeq = np.ones((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    Aeq[0, n] = 0.0

    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
    bounds.append((-1.0, None))

    try:
        res = linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=rhs, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64),
                      bounds=bounds, method="highs")
    except Exception:
        try:
            res = linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=rhs, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64),
                          bounds=bounds, method="revised simplex")
        except Exception:
            return None

    if res is None or getattr(res, "x", None) is None:
        return None
    xv = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    if xv.size != n + 1 or not np.all(np.isfinite(xv)):
        return None

    g = np.maximum(xv[:n], 0.0)
    sg = float(g.sum())
    if sg < 1e-15:
        return None
    g = g / sg
    return g, float(xv[n])


def lp_fw_shifted(a0, time_limit, initial_K=300, max_K=600, add_per_iter=25,
                  no_improve_limit=260, verbose=False):
    time_limit = max(1e-6, float(time_limit))
    a = norm_sum1(a0)
    n = len(a)

    c = fast_conv_same(a)
    best_obj = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
    best = a.copy()

    if n > 9000 or not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return best, best_obj

    if n <= 4500:
        max_K = min(max_K, 850)
    elif n <= 7500:
        max_K = min(max_K, 650)
    else:
        max_K = min(max_K, 450)

    initial_K = min(initial_K, max_K)
    active = set(int(k) for k in np.argsort(c)[::-1][:initial_K])

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    noimp = 0
    fail = 0
    t = 0

    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit and noimp < no_improve_limit:
        t += 1
        c = fast_conv_same(a)
        M = float(c.max())
        obj = float(2.0 * n * M)

        if obj < best_obj - 1e-11:
            best_obj = obj
            best = a.copy()
            noimp = 0
        else:
            noimp += 1

        if noimp > 90:
            active = set(int(k) for k in np.argsort(c)[::-1][:max_K])
            if noimp > 140:
                a = best.copy()
                c = fast_conv_same(a)
                M = float(c.max())
                obj = float(2.0 * n * M)
                continue

        if len(active) > max_K:
            active_list = list(active)
            margins = np.empty(len(active_list), dtype=np.float64)
            for i_idx, kk in enumerate(active_list):
                margins[i_idx] = M - float(c[int(kk)])
            keep = np.argsort(margins)[:max_K]
            newset = set()
            for i_idx in keep:
                newset.add(int(active_list[i_idx]))
            active = newset

        klist = sorted(active)
        sol = solve_lp_shifted(a, c, klist, n)
        if sol is None:
            fail += 1
            if fail > 6:
                break
            continue
        fail = 0
        g, t_lp = sol

        yg = fast_conv_xy(a, g)
        delta = yg - c
        top_d = np.argsort(delta)[::-1][:add_per_iter]
        for idx in top_d:
            active.add(int(idx))
        top_c = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:30]
        for idx in top_c:
            active.add(int(idx))

        best_trial_obj = obj
        best_trial = a
        alphas = (2.0 / (t + 2.0), 1.0 / (t + 1.0), 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.9, 1.0)
        for raw in alphas:
            alpha = float(raw)
            if alpha <= 0.0:
                continue
            if alpha > 1.0:
                alpha = 1.0
            tr = (1.0 - alpha) * a + alpha * g
            tr = np.maximum(tr, 0.0)
            st = float(tr.sum())
            if st < 1e-15:
                continue
            tr = tr / st
            c_tr = fast_conv_same(tr)
            obj_tr = float(2.0 * n * c_tr.max())
            if obj_tr < best_trial_obj - 1e-11:
                best_trial_obj = obj_tr
                best_trial = tr.copy()

        if best_trial_obj < obj - 1e-11:
            a = best_trial
            noimp = 0
        else:
            noimp += 1

        if verbose and (t % 75 == 0 or noimp % 100 == 0):
            print("  LP-FW t=" + str(t) + " obj=" + repr(best_obj) +
                  " elapsed=" + repr(time.monotonic() - t0) + "s")

    return best, best_obj


def trial_move(a, src, dst, frac):
    src = int(src)
    dst = int(dst)
    if src < 0 or dst < 0 or src >= len(a) or dst >= len(a) or src == dst:
        return None, float("inf")
    amt = float(a[src]) * float(frac)
    if amt <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(amt):
        return None, float("inf")
    tr = a.copy()
    tr[src] -= amt
    tr[dst] += amt
    tr = np.maximum(tr, 0.0)
    s = float(tr.sum())
    if s < 1e-15:
        return None, float("inf")
    tr = tr / s
    obj = fast_objective_normalized(tr)
    return tr, obj


def coordinate_polish(a0, time_limit, rng_seed=55):
    time_limit = max(1e-6, float(time_limit))
    a = norm_sum1(a0)
    n = len(a)
    c = fast_conv_same(a)
    best_obj = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
    best = a.copy()
    current_obj = best_obj
    rng = np.random.default_rng(rng_seed)

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    stall = 0

    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit and stall < 220:
        c = fast_conv_same(a)
        current_obj = float(2.0 * n * c.max())

        w = smooth_weights(c, 14)
        if w is None:
            break
        h = lag_cost(a, w)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
            break

        src_scores = a * h
        srcs = np.argsort(src_scores)[::-1][:8]
        dests = np.argsort(h)[:6]

        improved = False
        for s_idx in srcs:
            s_idx = int(s_idx)
            if a[s_idx] < 1e-13:
                continue
            for d_idx in dests:
                d_idx = int(d_idx)
                if d_idx == s_idx:
                    continue
                for frac in (0.01, 0.04, 0.12, 0.30):
                    tr, obj_tr = trial_move(a, s_idx, d_idx, frac)
                    if tr is None:
                        continue
                    if obj_tr < best_obj - 1e-11:
                        best_obj = obj_tr
                        best = tr.copy()
                    if obj_tr < current_obj - 1e-10:
                        a = tr.copy()
                        current_obj = obj_tr
                        improved = True
                        break
                if improved:
                    break
            if improved:
                break

        if improved:
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 80:
                base = best.copy()
                noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.015, n) * base
                a = base + noise
                a = 0.995 * a + 0.005 / float(n)
                a = norm_sum1(a)
                current_obj = fast_objective_normalized(a)
                stall = 0

    return best, best_obj


def softmax_vec(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    x = x - x.max()
    y = np.exp(x)
    s = float(y.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones_like(x, dtype=np.float64) / float(x.size)
    return y / s


def _lbfbs_fun(x, n, beta):
    a = softmax_vec(x)
    c = fast_conv_same(a)
    z = beta * c
    zmax = float(z.max())
    e = np.exp(z - zmax)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return 1e100
    return float(zmax / beta + np.log(s) / beta)


def _lbfbs_jac(x, n, beta):
    a = softmax_vec(x)
    c = fast_conv_same(a)
    z = beta * c
    zmax = float(z.max())
    e = np.exp(z - zmax)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.zeros_like(x, dtype=np.float64)
    lam = e / s
    h = lag_cost(a, lam)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
        return np.zeros_like(x, dtype=np.float64)
    ga = 2.0 * h
    sig = float(np.dot(ga, a))
    return ga - a * sig


def lbfbs_polish(a0, time_limit, beta=80.0):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return a0, fast_objective_normalized(a0)
    a = norm_sum1(a0)
    n = len(a)
    c0 = fast_conv_same(a)
    best_obj = float(2.0 * n * c0.max())
    best = a.copy()

    nlev = max(1, float(n))
    x0 = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-12))
    x0 -= x0.mean()

    if n <= 6000:
        maxfun = 360
        maxiter = 300
    elif n <= 12000:
        maxfun = 180
        maxiter = 150
    else:
        maxfun = 70
        maxiter = 60

    try:
        res = minimize(_lbfbs_fun, x0, args=(n, float(beta)), jac=_lbfbs_jac,
                       method="L-BFGS-B",
                       options={"maxfun": int(maxfun), "maxiter": int(maxiter), "iprint": 0})
    except Exception:
        return best, best_obj

    if getattr(res, "x", None) is None:
        return best, best_obj

    a2 = softmax_vec(res.x)
    obj2 = fast_objective_normalized(a2)
    if obj2 < best_obj - 1e-12:
        best = a2
        best_obj = obj2

    return best, best_obj


def evaluate_user(seq):
    try:
        vals = [float(x) for x in seq]
        return float(evaluate_sequence(vals))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def propose_candidate(budget_s=1800.0):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total = min(float(budget_s) - 35.0, 1765.0)
    if total < 60.0:
        total = 60.0

    MAX_N = 18000
    rng = np.random.default_rng(20240521)

    best_seq = None
    best_obj = float("inf")
    pool = []

    # Use provided previous construction if available.
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if p.ndim == 1 and p.size > 0:
            p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if p.sum() > 1e-12:
                if p.size > MAX_N:
                    p = interpolate_sequence(p, MAX_N)
                best_seq, best_obj = try_update_best(best_seq, best_obj, p, "parent", t0)
                add_pool(pool, p, best_obj, 10)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Select several lengths to explore.
    lengths = []
    if best_seq is not None:
        lengths.append(len(best_seq))
    for ell in (2500, 3500, 4500, 5500, 6700, 8000, 10000, 13000):
        if 1500 <= ell <= MAX_N:
            lengths.append(ell)

    uniq_lengths = []
    for ell in lengths:
        ok = True
        for u in uniq_lengths:
            if u == ell:
                ok = False
                break
        if ok:
            uniq_lengths.append(ell)

    scan_deadline = t0 + min(120.0, total * 0.08)
    for ell in uniq_lengths[:7]:
        if time.monotonic() >= scan_deadline:
            break
        best_seq, best_obj = scan_length(ell, rng, best_seq, best_obj, t0, scan_deadline, pool)

    if best_seq is None:
        n = 5000
        i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
        a = norm_sum1(i ** (-0.5))
        best_seq, best_obj = try_update_best(best_seq, best_obj, a, "default", t0)
        add_pool(pool, best_seq, best_obj, 10)

    # Stage: multiplicative-weight polish on several promising starts.
    tilt_deadline = t0 + min(540.0, total * 0.34)
    if time.monotonic() < tilt_deadline:
        cands = get_best_candidates(pool, 5, best_seq, best_obj)
        for cand in cands:
            rem = tilt_deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 35.0:
                break
            sub = min(rem, 170.0 if len(cand[0]) <= 8000 else 90.0)
            if sub > 10.0:
                best_seq, best_obj = tilt_polish(cand[0], sub, p_values=(8, 16, 32), floor=1e-8)
                add_pool(pool, best_seq, best_obj, 10)

        rem = tilt_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem > 60.0:
            best_seq, best_obj = tilt_polish(best_seq, min(rem, 220.0), p_values=(10, 24, 48), floor=1e-9)
            add_pool(pool, best_seq, best_obj, 10)

    # Stage: LP/Frank-Wolfe polish on moderate lengths.
    lp_deadline = t0 + min(800.0, total * 0.56)
    if HAVE_SCIPY and len(best_seq) <= 9000 and time.monotonic() < lp_deadline:
        rem = lp_deadline - time.monotonic()
        sub = min(rem, 320.0)
        if sub > 60.0:
            best_seq, best_obj = lp_fw_shifted(
                best_seq, sub, initial_K=320, max_K=650, add_per_iter=28,
                no_improve_limit=260, verbose=True
            )
            add_pool(pool, best_seq, best_obj, 10)

            rem = lp_deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem > 50.0:
                best_seq, best_obj = tilt_polish(best_seq, min(rem, 140.0), p_values=(12, 32), floor=1e-9)
                add_pool(pool, best_seq, best_obj, 10)

    # Stage: length perturbation / scaling.
    scale_deadline = t0 + min(1180.0, total * 0.83)
    if time.monotonic() < scale_deadline:
        bn = len(best_seq)
        targets = []
        for d in (800, 2000, 4000, 7000):
            tpar = bn + d
            if 2000 <= tpar <= MAX_N:
                targets.append(tpar)
        tpar = int(min(MAX_N, bn * 1.4))
        if 2000 <= tpar <= MAX_N:
            targets.append(tpar)
        for d in (1000, 2500, 5000):
            tpar = bn - d
            if 2000 <= tpar < bn:
                targets.append(tpar)

        unique_targets = []
        for tpar in targets:
            ok = True
            for u in unique_targets:
                if u == tpar:
                    ok = False
                    break
            if ok:
                unique_targets.append(int(tpar))

        for tpar in unique_targets[:5]:
            rem = scale_deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 45.0:
                break
            a_long = interpolate_sequence(best_seq, tpar)
            sub = min(rem, 130.0 if tpar <= 8000 else 80.0)
            if sub > 15.0:
                a_long, obj_long = tilt_polish(a_long, sub, p_values=(8, 16, 32), floor=1e-8)
                add_pool(pool, a_long, obj_long, 10)
                best_seq, best_obj = update_with_obj(best_seq, best_obj, a_long, obj_long, "scale_n_" + str(tpar), t0)

                rem2 = scale_deadline - time.monotonic()
                if tpar <= 8000 and HAVE_SCIPY and rem2 > 70.0:
                    a_long, obj_long = lp_fw_shifted(
                        a_long, min(rem2, 110.0), initial_K=280, max_K=520,
                        add_per_iter=22, no_improve_limit=160, verbose=False
                    )
                    best_seq, best_obj = update_with_obj(best_seq, best_obj, a_long, obj_long, "scale_lp_n_" + str(tpar), t0)

    final_deadline = t0 + max(60.0, total - 25.0)

    # Final coordinate polish.
    if time.monotonic() < final_deadline and len(best_seq) <= 12000:
        sub = min(final_deadline - time.monotonic(), 110.0 if len(best_seq) <= 7000 else 60.0)
        if sub > 25.0:
            best_seq, best_obj = coordinate_polish(best_seq, sub, rng_seed=73)
            add_pool(pool, best_seq, best_obj, 10)

    # L-BFGS smooth polish.
    if HAVE_SCIPY and time.monotonic() < final_deadline and len(best_seq) <= 16000:
        sub = min(final_deadline - time.monotonic(), 90.0 if len(best_seq) <= 8000 else 45.0)
        if sub > 35.0:
            best_seq, best_obj = lbfbs_polish(best_seq, sub, beta=80.0)
            add_pool(pool, best_seq, best_obj, 10)

            sub = final_deadline - time.monotonic()
            if sub > 35.0:
                best_seq, best_obj = lbfbs_polish(best_seq, sub, beta=160.0)
                add_pool(pool, best_seq, best_obj, 10)

    # Final small coordinate pass.
    if time.monotonic() < final_deadline and len(best_seq) <= 10000:
        sub = min(final_deadline - time.monotonic(), 45.0)
        if sub > 15.0:
            best_seq, best_obj = coordinate_polish(best_seq, sub, rng_seed=999)
            add_pool(pool, best_seq, best_obj, 10)

    # Optional internal exact evaluation for small enough final sequence.
    if len(best_seq) <= 8000 and time.monotonic() < t0 + total - 8.0:
        exact = evaluate_user(best_seq)
        if np.isfinite(exact):
            best_obj = min(best_obj, exact)

    # Sanitize final output.
    if best_seq is None:
        n = 5000
        i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
        best_seq = norm_sum1(i ** (-0.5))

    final = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    final = np.nan_to_num(final, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    final = np.maximum(final, 0.0)
    final = np.minimum(final, 1000.0)
    s = float(final.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        final = np.ones_like(final, dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, len(final)))

    final_obj = fast_objective_normalized(final)
    print("FINAL [" + "propose" + "] fast_obj=" + repr(final_obj) +
          " n=" + str(len(final)) + " elapsed=" + repr(time.monotonic() - t0) + "s")

    return [float(x) for x in final]
```
